liaison channels from the Central Intelligence Agency.

Preface

China: International Trade
Annual Statistical Supplement

The commodity data for China’s world trade presented in this publication
are estimated from the statistics of China’s trade partners. The data are
obtained by aggregating commodity data that have been categorized
according to the United Nations’ Standard International Trade Classifica-
tion, Revised (SITC, Revision 1).

The basic source of non-Communist trade partner data used in this report
is the United Nations, Statistical Papers, Series D, Commodity Trade
Statistics. Among Communist countries, only Czechoslovakia, Hungary,
and Yugoslavia report trade statistics to the United Nations. Data for the
USSR and for most other East European countries are obtained from the
official statistics of those countries. For countries that do not report official
trade statistics, data are occasionally derived from trade agreements and
other trade indicators.

Commodity values at each level of the SITC are adjusted in order to make
total exports and imports for each trade partner consistent with the totals
presented in tables 2 and 3 of the quarterly trade paper. Thus, both
Chinese exports and imports are presented on a free-on-board (f.o.b.) basis.
In some cases the official trade partner statistics are adjusted for
unrecorded trade where documentary evidence clearly indicates that the
official statistics underreport actual levels of trade.

Three different procedures are used to estimate commodity values for
countries that lag in reporting data or for countries for which data are in-
complete or nonexistent. First, for countries that report quantities but not
values, the values are estimated by multiplying the quantity data by
average prices for those commodities in China’s trade with other countries.
Second, for countries that have not reported commodity data in a given
year, values for that year are extrapolated from the most recent year for
which complete commodity data exist. Total values for missing years,
obtained from the tables in the quarterly trade paper, are distributed
proportionately over the commodities of the known base year. For 1983,
more than 98 percent of China’s total exports and 99 percent of total
imports are obtained either directly from reported trade statistics or by this

extrapolation procedure. 1 Finally, for countries that have never reported
commodity details in the period from 1970 to 1983, the small remainder is
distributed proportionately over the commodity values obtained from the
above procedures. 2

1 Footnotes in the following tables indicate shares of the totals obtained directly from
reported commodity trade statistics. The data base currently contains commodity data for
101 countries.

2 The residual, however, is not distributed over SITC categories 00 (Live Animals) and 331
(Crude Petroleum) for exports or over categories 04 (Cereals) and 7 (Machinery and
Transport Equipment) for imports. Virtually all of China’s trade in these commodities is
conducted with countries for which commodity trade statistics are available.
